Hi. Well, I'm here because... I'm just having a really hard time at work. I do bookkeeping, and I just can't stop going back over the same spreadsheets and calculations again and again.

I feel like I have to do it one more time. Because if I don't check it again, I get this overwhelming feeling that something really bad is going to happen, and it'll be entirely my fault.

It really is just the numbers for my job that I get completely stuck on.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or on edge when you're not thinking about work?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... it's hard to find a time when I'm not thinking about work lately, to be honest. But if I actually manage to distract myself and stop thinking about it for a minute, I don't feel anxious just out of nowhere. The on-edge feeling is pretty much always tied to worrying I messed up the numbers. If I'm not thinking about that, I feel pretty normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your body when you're feeling anxious about the numbers, like a racing heart or sweating?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean... my heart doesn't race, and I don't get sweaty or anything. It's really just the thoughts that won't stop. But physically, no, I don't think my body does anything weird when I get like that.\n\nDoctor: Has this issue with repeatedly checking numbers affected your relationships or other areas of your life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. I mean, at work, I actually missed a filing deadline recently because of it, and I got called into a formal meeting. And outside of work... I've basically stopped making it to my choir rehearsals and my volunteer commitments at the youth center. I just... I can't get out of the office on time anymore because I'm stuck there checking everything.\n\nDoctor: How upsetting or disturbing do you find these recurring thoughts about the numbers to be?\n\nPatient: It's really upsetting, honestly. Even after I check everything and I know for a fact the numbers match, this thought just keeps jumping into my head that I missed one hidden mistake somewhere in the payroll or the taxes. I start worrying that somebody's check won't go through, or the firm is going to get in serious trouble because of me. I try to just push the thought away and move on, but it comes right back. It's incredibly frustrating because it's like... I just can't trust my own eyes anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform any specific rituals or behaviors when you're not checking the numbers to try and reduce your anxiety?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't have any, like, special routines or anything like that. It's really just the checking. If I'm not checking the numbers, I'm just... sitting there worrying about them. But I don't do anything else to try and make it stop.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n10\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF42.2, F41.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I'm here because... I'm just having a really hard time at work. I do bookkeeping, and I just can't stop going back over the same spreadsheets and calculations again and again. \n\nIt's gotten to the point where I'm staying at the office for hours after everyone else leaves. Even when I've already checked the numbers and I know they're right, I just... I feel like I have to do it one more time. Because if I don't check it again, I get this overwhelming feeling that something really bad is going to happen, and it'll be entirely my fault.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these repetitive thoughts or urges to check things when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Well, it's mostly about work, but the thoughts definitely follow me home. Like, even after I leave the office and I know the numbers match, this thought just keeps jumping in. I'll be at home and suddenly think that I missed one hidden mistake in payroll or the taxes. I start worrying that somebody's check won't go through, or the firm will get in trouble. I try to push the thought away so I can just relax, but it comes right back. It's like... I just can't trust my own eyes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have similar worries or repetitive thoughts about things outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I haven't really noticed anything like that. It's pretty much just the bookkeeping stuff. I don't worry about checking things around my apartment or anything like that. It really is just the numbers for my job that I get completely stuck on.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or on edge when you're not thinking about work?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... it's hard to find a time when I'm not thinking about work lately, to be honest. But if I actually manage to distract myself and stop thinking about it for a minute, I don't feel anxious just out of nowhere. The on-edge feeling is pretty much always tied to worrying I messed up the numbers. If I'm not thinking about that, I feel pretty normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your body when you're feeling anxious about the numbers, like a racing heart or sweating?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean... my heart doesn't race, and I don't get sweaty or anything. It's really just the thoughts that won't stop. But physically, no, I don't think my body does anything weird when I get like that.\n\nDoctor: Has this issue with repeatedly checking numbers affected your relationships or other areas of your life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. I mean, at work, I actually missed a filing deadline recently because of it, and I got called into a formal meeting. And outside of work... I've basically stopped making it to my choir rehearsals and my volunteer commitments at the youth center. I just... I can't get out of the office on time anymore because I'm stuck there checking everything.\n\nDoctor: How upsetting or disturbing do you find these recurring thoughts about the numbers to be?\n\nPatient: It's really upsetting, honestly. Even after I check everything and I know for a fact the numbers match, this thought just keeps jumping into my head that I missed one hidden mistake somewhere in the payroll or the taxes. I start worrying that somebody's check won't go through, or the firm is going to get in serious trouble because of me. I try to just push the thought away and move on, but it comes right back. It's incredibly frustrating because it's like... I just can't trust my own eyes anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform any specific rituals or behaviors when you're not checking the numbers to try and reduce your anxiety?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't have any, like, special routines or anything like that. It's really just the checking. If I'm not checking the numbers, I'm just... sitting there worrying about them. But I don't do anything else to try and make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about checking the numbers are coming from your own mind and are something you want to be thinking about?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I know it's my own brain doing it. It's not like someone else is putting the thoughts there or anything. But I definitely do not want to be thinking about it. If I could just do my job and go home without worrying, I would in a heartbeat. I just... I don't really know why my mind gets stuck on it like this.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling like these thoughts and behaviors are taking a toll on your overall well-being or happiness?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is. I start worrying that somebody's check won't go through, or the firm will get in trouble. I try to push the thought away so I can just relax, but it comes right back. It's like... I just can't trust my own eyes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have similar worries or repetitive thoughts about things outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I haven't really noticed anything like that. It's pretty much just the bookkeeping stuff. I don't worry about checking things around my apartment or anything like that. I mean... it's hard to find a time when I'm not thinking about work lately, to be honest. The on-edge feeling is pretty much always tied to worrying I messed up the numbers. If I'm not thinking about that, I feel pretty normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your body when you're feeling anxious about the numbers, like a racing heart or sweating?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I don't really know why my mind gets stuck on it like this.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling like these thoughts and behaviors are taking a toll on your overall well-being or happiness?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I'm here because... I'm just having a really hard time at work. I do bookkeeping, and I just can't stop going back over the same spreadsheets and calculations again and again. \n\nIt's gotten to the point where I'm staying at the office for hours after everyone else leaves. Even when I've already checked the numbers and I know they're right, I just... I feel like I have to do it one more time. Because if I don't check it again, I get this overwhelming feeling that something really bad is going to happen, and it'll be entirely my fault.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these repetitive thoughts or urges to check things when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Well, it's mostly about work, but the thoughts definitely follow me home. Like, even after I leave the office and I know the numbers match, this thought just keeps jumping in. I'll be at home and suddenly think that I missed one hidden mistake in payroll or the taxes. I start worrying that somebody's check won't go through, or the firm will get in trouble. I try to push the thought away so I can just relax, but it comes right back. It's like... I just can't trust my own eyes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have similar worries or repetitive thoughts about things outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I haven't really noticed anything like that. It's pretty much just the bookkeeping stuff. I don't worry about checking things around my apartment or anything like that. It really is just the numbers for my job that I get completely stuck on.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or on edge when you're not thinking about work?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... it's hard to find a time when I'm not thinking about work lately, to be honest. But if I actually manage to distract myself and stop thinking about it for a minute, I don't feel anxious just out of nowhere. The on-edge feeling is pretty much always tied to worrying I messed up the numbers. If I'm not thinking about that, I feel pretty normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your body when you're feeling anxious about the numbers, like a racing heart or sweating?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean... my heart doesn't race, and I don't get sweaty or anything. It's really just the thoughts that won't stop. But physically, no, I don't think my body does anything weird when I get like that.\n\nDoctor: Has this issue with repeatedly checking numbers affected your relationships or other areas of your life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. I mean, at work, I actually missed a filing deadline recently because of it, and I got called into a formal meeting. And outside of work... I've basically stopped making it to my choir rehearsals and my volunteer commitments at the youth center. I just... I can't get out of the office on time anymore because I'm stuck there checking everything.\n\nDoctor: How upsetting or disturbing do you find these recurring thoughts about the numbers to be?\n\nPatient: It's really upsetting, honestly. Even after I check everything and I know for a fact the numbers match, this thought just keeps jumping into my head that I missed one hidden mistake somewhere in the payroll or the taxes. I start worrying that somebody's check won't go through, or the firm is going to get in serious trouble because of me. I try to just push the thought away and move on, but it comes right back. It's incredibly frustrating because it's like... I just can't trust my own eyes anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform any specific rituals or behaviors when you're not checking the numbers to try and reduce your anxiety?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't have any, like, special routines or anything like that. It's really just the checking. If I'm not checking the numbers, I'm just... sitting there worrying about them. But I don't do anything else to try and make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about checking the numbers are coming from your own mind and are something you want to be thinking about?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I know it's my own brain doing it. It's not like someone else is putting the thoughts there or anything. But I definitely do not want to be thinking about it. If I could just do my job and go home without worrying, I would in a heartbeat. I just... I don't really know why my mind gets stuck on it like this.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling like these thoughts and behaviors are taking a toll on your overall well-being or happiness?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is. It's affecting pretty much everything. I actually... well, I missed a filing deadline recently, and I got called into a formal meeting at work about it. And it's not just work. I've stopped making it to my choir rehearsals, and I can't do my commitments at the youth center anymore either. I just can't get out of the office on time to go to them. So, yeah, it's definitely taking a big toll on my life right now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other parts of your life, besides work and the activities you've mentioned, that you feel are being negatively impacted by these thoughts and behaviors?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, work and the choir and volunteering at the youth center... that pretty much makes up my whole schedule anyway. I don't really do much else. \n\nI guess I just go straight home now and kind of crash, mostly because I'm so exhausted from being stressed at the office all day. But as far as other activities or things like that... no, there isn't really anything else specific that comes to mind. It's just mainly the things I already mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble enjoying time with family or friends because of these thoughts about work?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I don't really have a lot of close friends right now anyway, and my family doesn't live nearby. I mean, I guess if I did have plans with people, I'd probably be too tired to go or I'd be late because I was stuck at the office. But since I don't really have those kinds of plans, it hasn't really been an issue.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I'm here because... I'm just having a really hard time at work. I do bookkeeping, and I just can't stop going back over the same spreadsheets and calculations again and again. \n\nIt's gotten to the point where I'm staying at the office for hours after everyone else leaves. Even when I've already checked the numbers and I know they're right, I just... I feel like I have to do it one more time. Because if I don't check it again, I get this overwhelming feeling that something really bad is going to happen, and it'll be entirely my fault.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these repetitive thoughts or urges to check things when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Well, it's mostly about work, but the thoughts definitely follow me home. Like, even after I leave the office and I know the numbers match, this thought just keeps jumping in. I'll be at home and suddenly think that I missed one hidden mistake in payroll or the taxes. I start worrying that somebody's check won't go through, or the firm will get in trouble. I try to push the thought away so I can just relax, but it comes right back. It's like... I just can't trust my own eyes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have similar worries or repetitive thoughts about things outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I haven't really noticed anything like that. It's pretty much just the bookkeeping stuff. I don't worry about checking things around my apartment or anything like that. It really is just the numbers for my job that I get completely stuck on.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or on edge when you're not thinking about work?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... it's hard to find a time when I'm not thinking about work lately, to be honest. But if I actually manage to distract myself and stop thinking about it for a minute, I don't feel anxious just out of nowhere. The on-edge feeling is pretty much always tied to worrying I messed up the numbers. If I'm not thinking about that, I feel pretty normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your body when you're feeling anxious about the numbers, like a racing heart or sweating?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean... my heart doesn't race, and I don't get sweaty or anything. It's really just the thoughts that won't stop. But physically, no, I don't think my body does anything weird when I get like that.\n\nDoctor: Has this issue with repeatedly checking numbers affected your relationships or other areas of your life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. I mean, at work, I actually missed a filing deadline recently because of it, and I got called into a formal meeting. And outside of work... I've basically stopped making it to my choir rehearsals and my volunteer commitments at the youth center. I just... I can't get out of the office on time anymore because I'm stuck there checking everything.\n\nDoctor: How upsetting or disturbing do you find these recurring thoughts about the numbers to be?\n\nPatient: It's really upsetting, honestly. Even after I check everything and I know for a fact the numbers match, this thought just keeps jumping into my head that I missed one hidden mistake somewhere in the payroll or the taxes. I start worrying that somebody's check won't go through, or the firm is going to get in serious trouble because of me. I try to just push the thought away and move on, but it comes right back. It's incredibly frustrating because it's like... I just can't trust my own eyes anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform any specific rituals or behaviors when you're not checking the numbers to try and reduce your anxiety?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't have any, like, special routines or anything like that. It's really just the checking. If I'm not checking the numbers, I'm just... sitting there worrying about them. But I don't do anything else to try and make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about checking the numbers are coming from your own mind and are something you want to be thinking about?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I know it's my own brain doing it. It's not like someone else is putting the thoughts there or anything. But I definitely do not want to be thinking about it. If I could just do my job and go home without worrying, I would in a heartbeat. I just... I don't really know why my mind gets stuck on it like this.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling like these thoughts and behaviors are taking a toll on your overall well-being or happiness?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is. But as far as other activities or things like that... no, there isn't really anything else specific that comes to mind. It's just mainly the things I already mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble enjoying time with family or friends because of these thoughts about work?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I start worrying that somebody's check won't go through, or the firm will get in trouble. I try to push the thought away so I can just relax, but it comes right back. It's like... I just can't trust my own eyes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have similar worries or repetitive thoughts about things outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... I haven't really noticed anything like that. It's pretty much just the bookkeeping stuff. I don't worry about checking things around my apartment or anything like that. It really is just the numbers for my job that I get completely stuck on.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or on edge when you're not thinking about work?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... it's hard to find a time when I'm not thinking about work lately, to be honest. But if I actually manage to distract myself and stop thinking about it for a minute, I don't feel anxious just out of nowhere. The on-edge feeling is pretty much always tied to worrying I messed up the numbers. If I'm not thinking about that, I feel pretty normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your body when you're feeling anxious about the numbers, like a racing heart or sweating?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean... my heart doesn't race, and I don't get sweaty or anything. It's really just the thoughts that won't stop. But physically, no, I don't think my body does anything weird when I get like that.\n\nDoctor: Has this issue with repeatedly checking numbers affected your relationships or other areas of your life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. I mean, at work, I actually missed a filing deadline recently because of it, and I got called into a formal meeting. And outside of work... I've basically stopped making it to my choir rehearsals and my volunteer commitments at the youth center. I just... I can't get out of the office on time anymore because I'm stuck there checking everything.\n\nDoctor: How upsetting or disturbing do you find these recurring thoughts about the numbers to be?\n\nPatient: It's really upsetting, honestly. Even after I check everything and I know for a fact the numbers match, this thought just keeps jumping into my head that I missed one hidden mistake somewhere in the payroll or the taxes. I start worrying that somebody's check won't go through, or the firm is going to get in serious trouble because of me. I try to just push the thought away and move on, but it comes right back. It's incredibly frustrating because it's like... I just can't trust my own eyes anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform any specific rituals or behaviors when you're not checking the numbers to try and reduce your anxiety?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't have any, like, special routines or anything like that. It's really just the checking. If I'm not checking the numbers, I'm just... sitting there worrying about them. But I don't do anything else to try and make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about checking the numbers are coming from your own mind and are something you want to be thinking about?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I know it's my own brain doing it. It's not like someone else is putting the thoughts there or anything. But I definitely do not want to be thinking about it. If I could just do my job and go home without worrying, I would in a heartbeat. I just... I don't really know why my mind gets stuck on it like this.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling like these thoughts and behaviors are taking a toll on your overall well-being or happiness?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is. I don't really do much else. \n\nI guess I just go straight home now and kind of crash, mostly because I'm so exhausted from being stressed at the office all day. But as far as other activities or things like that... no, there isn't really anything else specific that comes to mind. It's just mainly the things I already mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble enjoying time with family or friends because of these thoughts about work?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I don't really have a lot of close friends right now anyway, and my family doesn't live nearby. I mean, I guess if I did have plans with people, I'd probably be too tired to go or I'd be late because I was stuck at the office. But since I don't really have those kinds of plans, it hasn't really been an issue.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling like you're losing control over your life because of these thoughts and behaviors?\n\nPatient: I guess I haven't really thought about it exactly like that. I mean, I don't feel completely out of control, but... I definitely don't feel like I'm the one steering things right now. It's more like I'm just stuck in this loop at work and I can't figure out how to stop it. So maybe a little bit, yeah.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other ways, besides the impact on your work and the activities you've stopped doing, that you feel your life is being affected by these thoughts and behaviors?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. Like I said, it's mostly just work and those few activities I used to do. It's just that I literally don't have the time or energy for it because I'm stuck at the office. So, no, I wouldn't say the thoughts stop me from feeling pleasure or enjoying stuff... I just don't actually get to do those fun things right now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It really is just the numbers for my job that I get completely stuck on.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or on edge when you're not thinking about work?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean... it's hard to find a time when I'm not thinking about work lately, to be honest. But if I actually manage to distract myself and stop thinking about it for a minute, I don't feel anxious just out of nowhere. The on-edge feeling is pretty much always tied to worrying I messed up the numbers. If I'm not thinking about that, I feel pretty normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your body when you're feeling anxious about the numbers, like a racing heart or sweating?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean... my heart doesn't race, and I don't get sweaty or anything. It's really just the thoughts that won't stop. But physically, no, I don't think my body does anything weird when I get like that.\n\nDoctor: Has this issue with repeatedly checking numbers affected your relationships or other areas of your life outside of work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. I mean, at work, I actually missed a filing deadline recently because of it, and I got called into a formal meeting. And outside of work... I've basically stopped making it to my choir rehearsals and my volunteer commitments at the youth center. I just... I can't get out of the office on time anymore because I'm stuck there checking everything.\n\nDoctor: How upsetting or disturbing do you find these recurring thoughts about the numbers to be?\n\nPatient: It's really upsetting, honestly. Even after I check everything and I know for a fact the numbers match, this thought just keeps jumping into my head that I missed one hidden mistake somewhere in the payroll or the taxes. I start worrying that somebody's check won't go through, or the firm is going to get in serious trouble because of me. I try to just push the thought away and move on, but it comes right back. It's incredibly frustrating because it's like... I just can't trust my own eyes anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform any specific rituals or behaviors when you're not checking the numbers to try and reduce your anxiety?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't have any, like, special routines or anything like that. It's really just the checking. If I'm not checking the numbers, I'm just... sitting there worrying about them. But I don't do anything else to try and make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts about checking the numbers are coming from your own mind and are something you want to be thinking about?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I know it's my own brain doing it. It's not like someone else is putting the thoughts there or anything. But I definitely do not want to be thinking about it. If I could just do my job and go home without worrying, I would in a heartbeat. I just... I don't really know why my mind gets stuck on it like this.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling like these thoughts and behaviors are taking a toll on your overall well-being or happiness?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is.
